   - The shortest path from N to R through C is direct: N -> C -> R.

   - Node H is connected to nodes B, G, and I.

2. **Examine the immediate neighboring nodes:**
   - Node A's neighbors: E, B, S
   - Node H's neighbors: B, G, I

3. **Iteratively find other neighboring nodes:**
   - From E: No additional connections relevant to H.
   - From B: Connected to N, C, F, S, T, and H (already examined).
   - From S: Connected to F (already examined).
   - From G: Connected to T, I, Q, J, and R.
   - From I: Connected to P, Q, J, and R (already examined).

4. **Evaluate the found paths:**
   - Paths from A to H:
     - A -> B -> H
     - A -> B -> T -> G -> H
     - A -> B -> F -> T -> G -> H
     - A -> B -> C -> N -> O -> C -> B -> T -> G -> H
     - A -> B -> C -> B -> T -> G -> H
     - A -> B -> N -> O -> C -> B -> T -> G -> H
     - A -> B -> F -> T -> G -> H
     - A -> E -> B -> T -> G -> H
     - A -> S -> F -> T -> G -> H

5. **Identify the bottleneck:**
   - The edges (A, B) and (B, H) are critical in many paths.
   - Removing (A, B) disconnects A from the majority of the graph, but not fully from H.
   - Removing (B, H) disconnects H from the majority of the graph, but not fully from A.
   - Removing (B, T) and (T, G) will disconnect the central part of the graph, isolating A and H from each other.

6. **Check the minimum set of edges to remove:**
   - Removing (A, B) and (B, H) does not fully disconnect A and H.
   - Removing (B, T) and (T, G) will effectively disconnect A and H.
   - Removing (B, T) and (B, F) will also disconnect A and H.

After evaluating the paths and the necessary edges to remove, the minimum number of edges that must be removed is 2.

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(B, T), (T, G)].

--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed so that the two black nodes (S and O) are completely disconnected, we first need to identify all paths between these two nodes and find the bottleneck nodes that need to be removed to break all these paths.

1. **Identify the black nodes and their immediate neighbors:**
    - Node S is connected to nodes E and B.
    - Node O is connected to nodes R, J, and Q.

2. **Explore the paths from S to O:**
    - From S, the routes extend to E and B. We now look at routes from E and B.
    - From B, we can go to F, T, G, and E.

3. **Follow the paths from B:**
    - Path from B to F leads to no further connections towards O.
    - Path from B to T goes to F.
    - Path from B to G goes to C, N, and J.

4. **Follow the paths from G:**
   - From G, we reach C and J.
   - Path from C leads to N and R.
   - Path from J leads to O (a direct path to O from G’s neighbors).

5. **Explore the paths from E:**
   - From E, the routes extend to A and S.
   - From A, there is no connection to O.
   - From S goes to B, forming paths already identified.

6. **Analyze paths involving node O:**
   - Additionally, O connects to R and Q, and also connects back to J through node G/C.
   - Q is isolated and does not contribute to any S-O path.

7. **Find all unique paths:**
   - From S to B to G to J to O.
   - From S to B to C to N to R to O.
   - From S to B to G to C to R to O.
   - From S to B to G to J to O (redundant path through G-J).

8. **Find bottlenecks (nodes whose removal will break all paths):**
   - Removing G disconnects both paths through G.
   - Removing R disconnects paths through R.
   - Removing either E or B would break the connection from S and make it harder to reach other nodes, causing a need to remove more nodes.

  Therefore, the minimal set of nodes to remove, i.e., the minimal cut set, to ensure S and O are disconnected:
  Nodes to remove: 2
  Cut Set: [G, B]

Hence, the answer is:
Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [G, R]

### Step-by-Step Analysis:

1. **Identify the Black Nodes:**
   - The black nodes are \( S \) and \( O \).

2. **Find Neighbors of \( S \):**
   - \( S \) is directly connected to \( E \) and \( B \).

3. **Find Neighbors of \( O \):**
   - \( O \) is directly connected to \( J \) and \( R \).

1. **Identify the paths between S and R:**

   - Path 1: S -> B -> C -> G -> J -> O -> R
   - Path 2: S -> B -> C -> G -> J -> I -> H -> F -> T -> E -> A -> S (loop back to S, but we consider the segment until it intersects)
   - Path 3: S -> B -> C -> N -> R
   - Path 4: S -> A -> E -> B -> C -> O -> R
   - Path 5: S -> B -> T -> F -> H -> I -> J -> O -> R
   - Path 6: S -> A -> E -> B -> G -> J -> O -> R

2. **Find the bottleneck edges:**

   - Removing the edge (B, C) would disconnect several paths, but not all.
   - Removing the edge (J, O) would also disconnect several paths, but not all.
   - Removing the edge (C, N) would disconnect the path through N.
   - Removing the edge (S, B) would disconnect S from several paths.
   - Removing the edge (R, O) would disconnect R from several paths.

3. **Identify the minimum cut set:**

   - To completely disconnect S and R, we need to remove edges that cover all possible paths.
   - The edges (S, B), (B, C), and (J, O) together cover all paths between S and R.

Therefore, the minimum number of edges that must be removed is 3.

**Answer:**

1. **Identify the black nodes**: The black nodes are labeled A and R.
2. **Find the neighbors of node A**: The direct neighbors of A are E, B, and S.
3. **Find the neighbors of node R**: The direct neighbors of R are C, N, O, and G.
4. **Expand the search from A's neighbors**:
   - From E: The neighbors are A, F, B.
   - From B: The neighbors are A, E, S, F, T.
   - From S: The neighbors are A, B.
5. **Expand the search from R's neighbors**:
   - From C: The neighbors are R, N, G.
   - From N: The neighbors are C, R.
   - From O: The neighbors are R, J, Q.
   - From G: The neighbors are C, J, R.

6. **Continue expanding the search until a common node is found**:
   - From F: The neighbors are E, B, T.
   - From T: The neighbors are B, F.
   - From J: The neighbors are G, O, I, Q.
   - From I: The neighbors are J, H, P.
   - From Q: The neighbors are O, J, P.
   - From P: The neighbors are Q, I.

From the expansions, we see that B connects to T, and G connects to J. Both B and G are connected through intermediate nodes. We need to find a path touching both black nodes.

7. **Find a common path between any of the expanded nodes**:
   - From G (neighbor of R), we trace back to J.
   - From J, we trace back to O (neighbor of R).
   - From O, we trace back to Q.
   - From Q, we trace back to J.
   - From J, we trace back to I.
   - From I, we trace back to H.
   - From H, we trace back to G.
   - From G, we trace back to C.
   - From C, we trace back to N.
   - From N, we trace back to R.

Thus, a path from A to R is:
   - A → E → B → F → T → G → C → N → R
   However, it will be too long. Instead, looking back from the shortest path:

   - The shortest path from A we see is:
   - A → E → B
   - Now we connect from B to G's cloned path.

8. **The shortest path found**:
   - The path from A to R is:
   - A → E → B → F → T → G → C → R.

The result path seems too long. Now looking for the shortest path between E and G:

   - A → E → B → F → T → G
     Then, G → R

Finally, the minimal path found:
   - A → E → B
     B → F (additional feasible minimal path tracing back to G → N)
     G -> R

The verified minimal path found:
A → E → B → G → R

Length: 3, Path: [A, E, B, G, R]

   - Removing both C and T will disconnect G and N, but we need to check for potential bottlenecks.

   - We must check if there's any other single node that could achieve the same result, which isn't the case here due to multiple redundant paths.

8. **Final Check:**
   - Removing both C and T ensures that no path exists between G and N.
   - There isn't a single node removal that can achieve the disconnection due to the redundant paths.

Therefore, the minimum number of nodes that must be removed is 2.

7. Path: \( S \to F \to A \to E \to B \to T \to G \to Q \to R \)

- \( G \): Detect that the majority of paths involve \( G \).
- \( T \): Disconnection path would break from \( G \) to \( T \).
- \( B \): Additional paths evolve from \( B \) into \( T \).

1. **Identify the black nodes:**
   - Black nodes: E and J.

2. **Find the immediate neighbors of the black nodes:**
   - Node E is connected to: B, F, S.
   - Node J is connected to: F, G, Q, T.

3. **Trace the paths between E and J:**
   - Path 1: E -> F -> J
   - Path 2: E -> F -> G -> J
   - Path 3: E -> F -> T -> J
   - Path 4: E -> F -> G -> Q -> J

4. **Identify the edges that need to be removed to disconnect E and J:**
   - Removing edge (E, F) will disconnect E from F, but J still has other paths to reach E through G, T, and Q.
   - Removing edge (F, J) will disconnect F from J, but E still has other paths to reach J through G, T, and Q.
   - Removing edge (F, G) will disconnect F from G, but E and J still have other paths to reach each other through T and Q.
   - To completely disconnect E and J, we need to remove edges (F, J), (F, T), and (G, Q).

### Minimum Cut Set:
- Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(F, J), (F, T), (G, Q)].

This set of edges ensures that there are no paths remaining between the black nodes E and J.

--- Model Response ---
To dissect the graph and determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (R and J), we need to follow these steps:

1. **Identify the two black nodes**: The black nodes are R and J.
2. **Trace all paths between R and J**: Identify all intermediate nodes and edges connecting R and J.
3. **Determine the essential edges**: Find the minimum set of edges that, when removed, will disconnect R and J.

### Step-by-Step Analysis:
1. **Identify the black nodes**:
   - R is connected to nodes C, G, and J.
   - J is connected to nodes R, I, O, and Q.

2. **Trace paths from R to J**:
   - Direct path: (R, J)
   - Path through C: (R, C) -> (C, B) -> (B, J)
   - Path through G: (R, G) -> (G, H) -> (H, I) -> (I, J)
   - Path through O: (J, O) -> (O, Q) -> (Q, P) [Note: There's no direct connection to R here, so this path won't help]

3. **Identify critical edges**:
   - Removing (R, J) will disconnect R and J directly.
   - Removing (R, C) and (C, B) will disconnect R from B, but we need to ensure B is not a bridge from another path.
   - Removing (R, G) and (G, H) will disconnect R from H, but we need to ensure G is not a bridge from another path.
   - Removing (H, I) will disconnect H from I, ensuring this path from R to J is disconnected.
   - Removing (B, J) will disconnect B from J, ensuring this path from R to J is disconnected.

### Conclusion:
- We need to remove the following edges to ensure complete disconnection:
  - (R, J)
  - (H, I)
  - (B, J)

This results in:

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(R, J), (H, I), (B, J)].
--- Ground Truth ---
